# WorkOrch Framework / EventHub Module Structure Migration Plan

## Purpose

This document records the migration plan after comparing WorkOrch with `/home/rangh/codespace/magicRunner` under:

- `github.com/muidea/magicCommon/framework`
- `github.com/muidea/magicCommon/event`

The goal is to make WorkOrch module organization closer to Go project conventions and magicCommon lifecycle conventions while preserving WorkOrch's stronger owner-state and EventHub command boundaries.

This is a migration and verification checklist. It should be used to migrate one area at a time and record closure evidence.

## Reference Comparison

### magicRunner patterns to reuse

- Entrypoint uses framework application directly:
  - `application.Startup(ctx, service.HoldService())`
  - `application.Run(ctx)`
  - `application.Shutdown(ctx)`
- Entrypoint side-effect imports explicitly enable required initiators and modules.
- External infrastructure initiators are narrow:
  - `persistence`
  - `routeregistry`
  - `pprof`
- `internal/pkg/moduledeps` centralizes initiator lookups and fail-fast dependency resolution.
- `kernel/system` owns startup governance such as default namespace registration.
- Modules keep their own `biz`, `service`, `pkg`, and optional `internal` directories.

### magicRunner patterns not to copy directly

- magicRunner is mostly a route/service-oriented application. WorkOrch is a long-running governed orchestration runtime with formal owner state.
- WorkOrch should not let application/appservice access owner repositories or services directly just because magicRunner resolves a base client.
- WorkOrch should preserve EventHub command/response for owner-state reads, writes, and state-machine transitions.
- WorkOrch's split between `plugin.go` framework adapters and `module.go` explicit `Configure/Start/Stop` lifecycle is acceptable because WorkOrch has embedded runtime and test lifecycle needs.

## Current WorkOrch Baseline

### Current strengths

- `application/workorch/main.go` explicitly imports the selected initiator and modules.
- `plugin.go` wrapper cleanup is complete: modules register concrete `New()` implementations directly.
- Major owner modules are grouped under:
  - `internal/modules/application`
  - `internal/modules/blocks`
  - `internal/modules/kernel`
- Cross-owner production access has moved toward EventHub command/response.
- Local embedded runtime uses explicit lifecycle instead of scanning the global plugin registry.

### Remaining gaps

- WorkOrch now depends on `github.com/muidea/magicCommon v1.5.9`. The `application/workorch` entry runtime uses `application.StartupWithOptions` for explicit config/service-name injection and aliases its mode services to `framework/service.LifecycleService`, avoiding a duplicate local lifecycle interface.
- `internal/modules/application/workorchd/biz/appservice` is still a facade package with a broad public surface for HTTP/CLI stability. Focused packages now carry most pure logic, runtime session lifecycle, read-model reader contracts, read-model progress/context composition, and EventHub-backed owner ports, but some orchestration facades still need gradual splitting.
- Phase 8 skill refresh has been completed for the project-local skills and fed back to the local skill-hub default repository.

## Module Classification Review

The current names `application`, `blocks`, and `kernel` can remain, but module definitions should be tightened according to the actual responsibility each unit carries.

### Initiators

| Unit | Current role | Target classification | Adjustment |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `internal/initiators/workorchruntime` | Repository construction, EventHub wrapper, runtime policy | Infrastructure runtime initiator | Keep only infrastructure/runtime dependency export. Builtin defaults now belong to `kernel/system`; avoid dependency on kernel module models. |

Initiators are not owner modules. They should provide foundational dependencies and fail-fast dependency resolution only.

### Application modules

| Unit | Current role | Target classification | Adjustment |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `internal/services/workorch` | Process service; CLI/TUI behavior lives in `service/cli`, `service/tui`, `biz/backends`, `biz/agent`, and `biz/session` | `framework/service` lifecycle service, not a plugin module | Keep as ordinary process service imported by `application/workorch/main.go`; do not register it through `framework/plugin/module`. |
| `internal/modules/application/workorchd` | HTTP service host, runtime session, CLI dispatcher, read/app services | Application service host | Keep as application module. Split large `biz/appservice` package by use-case over time. It should not own kernel formal state. |

Application modules should compose user-facing services and use EventHub-backed ports for owner state.

Removal evidence for the old `application/workorch` plugin shape:

- The old module lifecycle had no formal owner state and no concrete plugin responsibility.
- `application/workorch/main.go` now imports `github.com/muidea/workorch/internal/services/workorch` directly as the process service.
- Side-effect imports in `application/workorch/main.go` are reserved for real initiator/module/block registrations.
- Real CLI/TUI behavior is executed by the process service, `biz/backends`, `biz/agent`, `biz/session`, `service/cli`, and `service/tui`, not by a framework plugin module.

Therefore `application/workorch` should be treated as the executable entrypoint namespace, while `internal/services/workorch` is the process lifecycle service.

### Blocks

| Unit | Current role | Target classification | Adjustment |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `blocks/file` | Runtime file list/read/edit adapter | Runtime capability block | Keep. |
| `blocks/git` | Runtime git read/write adapter | Runtime capability block | Keep. |
| `blocks/shell` | Runtime shell command adapter | Runtime capability block | Keep. |
| `blocks/ssh` | Runtime SSH command adapter | Runtime capability block | Keep. |
| `blocks/web` | Runtime web search/fetch adapter plus approval read command | Runtime capability block | Keep, but keep governance/approval state access through EventHub only. |
| `blocks/llm` | LLM workspace execution and provider command handling | Runtime/provider capability block | Keep as block. Document that it owns provider execution, not run state. |
| `blocks/mcp` | Runtime MCP tool/resource/prompt execution | Runtime capability block | Keep, but distinguish clearly from `kernel/mcp`. Optional later rename: `blocks/mcp_runtime` or `blocks/mcpclient` if ambiguity keeps causing mistakes. |

Blocks should not own formal WorkOrch state. They execute external or technical capabilities and return runtime evidence.

### Kernel owner/resource modules

| Unit | Current role | Target classification | Adjustment |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `kernel/workspace` | Workspace, goal, DoD owner | Formal resource owner | Keep. |
| `kernel/run` | Run, step, checkpoint owner and run state transitions | Formal resource/state-machine owner | Keep. |
| `kernel/result` | Result and artifact owner | Formal resource owner | Keep. |
| `kernel/memory` | Memory items and recall traces | Formal resource owner | Keep. |
| `kernel/soul` | Soul catalog and capability validation | Catalog/resource owner | Keep. |
| `kernel/skill` | Skill catalog | Catalog/resource owner | Keep. |
| `kernel/mcp` | MCP server and capability catalog | Catalog/resource owner | Keep, but define as catalog owner only; runtime execution stays in `blocks/mcp`. |
| `kernel/eventstream` | Event record projection/query/purge | Observability owner | Keep. |

These modules own repositories and expose EventHub command/read contracts.

### Kernel orchestration/governance modules

| Unit | Current role | Target classification | Adjustment |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `kernel/orchestrator` | Cross-owner runtime state orchestration and evidence handling | Orchestration/governance module | Keep. It can coordinate multiple owners but must use EventHub adapters for other owner state. |
| `kernel/decision` | Decision tickets and resolutions | Governance owner | Keep. |
| `kernel/approval` | Approval records and approval state | Governance owner | Keep. |
| `kernel/governance` | Auto governance trace/evaluation and continuation behavior | Governance orchestration module | Keep, but make long-running continuation/reconcile behavior use BackgroundRoutine and keep traces as its owned state. |
| `kernel/system` | Not currently present | Startup/system governance module | Add. Move builtin defaults and startup readiness checks here. |

This classification makes `kernel` contain both formal resource owners and governance/orchestration owners. That is acceptable if the individual module responsibility is explicit.

### Optional future regrouping

Do not rename directories immediately. If future complexity grows, introduce subgroups only after contracts are stable:

- `internal/modules/kernel/resources/...`
- `internal/modules/kernel/governance/...`
- `internal/modules/kernel/system/...`
- `internal/modules/blocks/runtime/...`

This is optional and higher risk because import paths and docs would churn. The near-term migration should tighten definitions first, not rename directories.

## Target Structure

### Entrypoint

`application/workorch/main.go` remains the only production side-effect import surface.

Expected responsibility:

- Process signal context.
- Program/version output if needed.
- Explicit initiator/module imports.
- Delegate mode parsing and app behavior to `internal/services/workorch`.

No appservice, bootstrap, server, repository, or owner implementation package should be imported from the entrypoint.

### Initiator

`internal/initiators/workorchruntime` should become an infrastructure-only runtime initiator.

Allowed responsibilities:

- Read effective configuration.
- Build repository provider/factory.
- Expose framework-provided EventHub as WorkOrch event wrapper.
- Expose runtime policy through an infrastructure contract package.
- Expose BackgroundRoutine if later needed by module dependency resolver.

Responsibilities to remove:

- Builtin defaults initialization.
- Owner-state policy decisions.
- Cross-owner service registry.
- Business startup governance.

### Shared dependency resolver

Add `internal/pkg/workorchdeps`, inspired by magicRunner `internal/pkg/moduledeps`.

Expected responsibilities:

- Resolve `workorchruntime` helper with `initiator.GetEntity`.
- Provide fail-fast helpers:
  - `ResolveRuntimeHelper(consumer string)`
  - `ResolveEventHub(consumer string)`
  - `ResolveRuntimePolicy(consumer string)`
  - `ResolveRepositoryProvider(consumer string)`
- Avoid exposing cross-owner services.
- Keep dependency errors explicit and centralized.

### Runtime policy contract

Move runtime policy model away from `kernel/orchestrator/pkg/models`.

Candidate locations:

- `internal/pkg/workorchruntimepolicy`
- `internal/pkg/runtimecontract`

Expected result:

- initiator depends only on `internal/pkg/...`
- orchestrator depends on the same shared policy contract
- no foundational package imports a kernel module model

### Runtime execution contracts

Move cross-module runtime execution contracts away from `kernel/orchestrator/pkg/models`.

Target location:

- `internal/pkg/workorchruntimecontract`

Contracts included:

- `StepExecutionRequest`
- `StepExecutionResult`
- `BlockExecutionGraph`
- `BlockExecutionNode`
- `ArtifactContinuation`
- `ArtifactContinuationChunk`
- `ArtifactContinuationAssembledMetadata`

Expected result:

- blocks depend on shared runtime contracts, not orchestrator module models
- repository interfaces and implementations depend on shared runtime contracts, not orchestrator module models
- appservice read models can consume artifact continuation data without importing orchestrator module models
- `internal/modules/kernel/orchestrator/pkg/models` is removed unless a future orchestrator-only model is needed

### System module

Add or formalize `internal/modules/kernel/system`.

Expected responsibilities:

- Ensure builtin defaults:
  - workspace templates
  - souls
  - MCP servers
  - MCP capabilities
- Perform startup governance checks.
- Publish startup or configuration readiness events if needed.

Lifecycle:

- `Setup`: resolve only required repository/provider/EventHub dependencies.
- `Run`: execute default initialization and readiness checks.
- `Teardown`: release subscriptions/resources if any.

Ordering:

- After `workorchruntime` initiator.
- Before owner modules that rely on builtin defaults at runtime.

### Owner modules

Kernel owner modules remain authoritative for their formal state.

Expected owner modules include:

- `workspace`
- `run`
- `orchestrator`
- `decision`
- `approval`
- `result`
- `memory`
- `soul`
- `skill`
- `mcp`
- `governance`
- `eventstream`

Rules:

- Owner repository/service access stays inside the owner module.
- Other modules use EventHub command/response.
- Command handlers return `events.Response`.
- Missing response is treated as lifecycle or wiring failure.

### Blocks

Blocks remain technical/runtime capabilities:

- `file`
- `git`
- `shell`
- `ssh`
- `llm`
- `mcp`
- `web`

Rules:

- Blocks do not own formal WorkOrch state.
- Runtime block execution uses EventHub topics.
- Provider configuration should live in block `pkg/providerconfig` or shared config packages.
- Long-running background work must use framework BackgroundRoutine or a module-owned cancellable lifecycle.

### Event contracts

Split `internal/pkg/events/topics.go` by domain or move owner-specific contracts closer to modules.

Acceptable migration options:

Option A: Keep package `internal/pkg/events`, split files:

- `topics_workspace.go`
- `topics_run.go`
- `topics_result.go`
- `topics_governance.go`
- `topics_blocks.go`
- `payload_keys.go`

Option B: Move owner-specific contracts to module packages:

- `internal/modules/kernel/run/pkg/events`
- `internal/modules/kernel/workspace/pkg/events`
- `internal/modules/kernel/result/pkg/events`
- `internal/modules/blocks/llm/pkg/events`

If using Option B, avoid circular imports and keep shared envelope/response helpers in `internal/pkg/events`.

Preferred first step: Option A, because it is lower risk and preserves current import paths.

### Command DTOs

Reduce use of service-layer command structs in EventHub payloads.

Target:

- Define stable command DTOs in `pkg/contracts` or `pkg/events`.
- Appservice and owner module both import the contract package.
- Owner `service` package remains internal to owner implementation.

Example target packages:

- `internal/modules/kernel/run/pkg/contracts`
- `internal/modules/kernel/workspace/pkg/contracts`
- `internal/modules/kernel/result/pkg/contracts`

## Migration Phases

### Phase 0: Remove no-op application/workorch plugin module

Tasks:

- Import `github.com/muidea/workorch/internal/services/workorch` normally from `application/workorch/main.go`.
- Keep side-effect imports only for real initiator/module/block registrations.
- Ensure `internal/services/workorch` has no `plugin.go` or `module.go` registration file.
- Keep `biz`, `service`, and `pkg` packages as ordinary process service implementation packages.
- Recheck any source-module constants that intentionally use `application/workorch`; keep event source constants if they identify CLI/application origin rather than plugin module identity.

Verification:

```bash
rg -n '_ "github.com/muidea/workorch/internal/services/workorch"' application internal -g '*.go'
rg -n 'pluginmodule\.Register\(New\(\)\)|type Module struct\{\}|func \(m \*Module\) Configure\(\)|func \(m \*Module\) Start\(\)' internal/services/workorch -g '*.go'
go test ./application/workorch ./internal/services/workorch/... -count=1
go test ./internal/initiators/... ./internal/modules/... ./internal/pkg/... -count=1
```

Expected result:

- CLI/TUI behavior still runs through `app.Run`.
- Framework plugin lifecycle no longer contains a no-op `application/workorch` module.
- `application/workorchd` remains the application service host module.

### Phase 1: Dependency resolver and initiator cleanup

Tasks:

- Create `internal/pkg/workorchdeps`.
- Replace repeated `initiator.GetEntity(runtimecommon.InitiatorID, helper)` calls in plugin adapters with `workorchdeps`.
- Move runtime policy model out of `kernel/orchestrator/pkg/models`.
- Update `workorchruntime` to expose the new policy contract.
- Move shared runtime execution and artifact continuation contracts out of `kernel/orchestrator/pkg/models`.

Verification:

```bash
rg -n 'initiator\.GetEntity\(runtimecommon\.InitiatorID' internal/modules internal/modules/application application -g '*.go' --glob '!**/*_test.go'
rg -n 'internal/modules/kernel/orchestrator/pkg/models' internal/initiators internal/pkg -g '*.go'
rg -n 'internal/modules/kernel/orchestrator/pkg/models|orchestrator/pkg/models' internal application -g '*.go'
go test ./internal/initiators/... ./internal/modules/... ./internal/pkg/... -count=1
```

Expected result:

- Production module plugins no longer duplicate runtime helper lookup logic.
- No initiator or shared foundational package imports orchestrator module models.
- Blocks, repository, workorchrepo, appservice, and orchestrator import shared runtime contracts from `internal/pkg/workorchruntimecontract`.

### Phase 2: Move builtin defaults to system module

Tasks:

- Add `internal/modules/kernel/system`.
- Move `workorchdefaults.EnsureBuiltinDefaults` call out of `workorchruntime.Run`.
- Wire system module through `application/workorch/main.go`.
- Assign system module weight before modules that require defaults.

Verification:

```bash
rg -n 'EnsureBuiltinDefaults' internal/initiators internal/modules -g '*.go' --glob '!**/*_test.go'
rg -n '_ "github.com/muidea/workorch/internal/modules/kernel/system"' application/workorch/main.go
go test ./internal/modules/kernel/system ./internal/initiators/... ./internal/modules/... -count=1
WORKORCH_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:8080 make verify-ci-mvp-file
```

Expected result:

- Defaults initialization belongs to a module, not the initiator.
- CI deployed-instance checks still pass.

### Phase 3: Repository access narrowing

Tasks:

- Introduce repository provider or grouped repository accessors.
- Update module plugin setup to request only the repositories each module owns.
- Keep appservice owner-state access through EventHub-backed read ports.

Verification:

```bash
rg -n 'runtime\.Repositories\(\)|ResolveRuntimeHelper\(ModuleID\)' internal/modules/application internal/modules/blocks internal/modules/kernel -g '*.go' --glob '!**/*_test.go'
rg -n '\.Repositories\.(Workspaces|Goals|DoDs|Runs|Steps|Results|Artifacts|Tickets|Approvals|Souls|Skills|MCPServers|MCPCapabilities)' internal/modules/application -g '*.go' --glob '!**/*_test.go'
go test ./internal/modules/... ./internal/pkg/... -count=1
```

Expected result:

- Application code does not use owner repositories.
- Modules do not receive unrelated repositories during setup.

### Phase 4: Event topic organization

Tasks:

- Split `internal/pkg/events/topics.go` into domain files without changing exported names.
- Keep `Envelope`, `Hub`, `Response`, and shared helpers in shared event package.
- Add comments or grouping for command vs notification topics.

Verification:

```bash
go test ./internal/pkg/events ./internal/modules/... -count=1
rg -n 'const \(' internal/pkg/events
```

Expected result:

- No behavior change.
- Event contract files are easier to navigate by owner/module.

### Phase 5: Command DTO cleanup

Tasks:

- Add owner contract packages for high-traffic commands first:
  - run
  - workspace
  - decision
  - approval
  - memory
  - soul
  - skill
  - mcp
- Replace payloads carrying owner `service` command structs with contract DTOs.
- Keep stable key/model fallback parsing where command payload is intentionally field-based; do not accept service-layer command structs as EventHub command contracts.

Verification:

```bash
rg -n 'PayloadKeyCommand.*service|PayloadKeyCommand].*\(.*service|\.Payload\[events\.PayloadKeyCommand\].*\(.*service' internal/modules internal/pkg -g '*.go' --glob '!**/*_test.go'
go test ./internal/modules/kernel/run ./internal/modules/kernel/workspace ./internal/modules/kernel/result ./internal/modules/kernel/decision ./internal/modules/kernel/approval ./internal/modules/kernel/memory ./internal/modules/kernel/soul ./internal/modules/kernel/skill ./internal/modules/kernel/mcp ./internal/modules/application/workorchd/biz/appservice -count=1
```

Expected result:

- Cross-module event contracts do not depend on owner service-layer command types.
- Owner service packages remain implementation details.

### Phase 6: Appservice package split

Tasks:

- Keep `internal/modules/application/workorchd/biz/appservice` as a thin HTTP/CLI-facing facade and split responsibilities only where the split strengthens module ownership, EventHub owner boundaries, or maintainability.
- Treat the following as mandatory closure criteria:
  - appservice production code must not import kernel owner `biz`, `service`, or owner repositories directly;
  - appservice must use EventHub-backed ports for owner-state reads, writes, state transitions, evidence lookup, and purge coordination;
  - EventHub command payloads must use stable contract DTOs or primitive payload maps, not owner service command types;
  - focused packages must not introduce reverse dependencies that let owner modules depend on appservice facade code;
  - runtime/session/bootstrap wiring must keep framework lifecycle assembly in application/bootstrap or initiator exports, not hidden in use-case helpers.
- Treat remaining file-size or same-package helper extraction as optional cleanup. Do it only when a file mixes distinct responsibilities or is likely to invite future EventHub/framework boundary violations; do not mechanically split state-machine, activation, or lifecycle orchestration code just to reduce line count.
- Candidate subpackages:
  - `activationports`
  - `appmodelcore`
  - `configcore`
  - `contentcore`
  - `contentports`
  - `contextregistryports`
  - `definitionports`
  - `governancecore`
  - `governanceports`
  - `localizationcore`
  - `qualitycore`
  - `readmodelcore`
  - `readmodelports`
  - `readmodelprojection`
  - `ownercommandports`
  - `ownerreadports`
  - `runports`
  - `runtimeports`
  - `runtime` / `runtimecore`
  - `skillselectcore`
  - `setupdraftports`
  - `readmodel`
  - `workspace`
  - `workspaceports`
  - `governance`
  - `cli`
  - `ports`
- Keep HTTP server imports stable through a small facade if needed.

Verification:

```bash
find internal/modules/application/workorchd/biz -maxdepth 2 -type d | sort
rg -n 'internal/modules/application/workorchd/biz/appservice' internal/modules/application/workorchd/service -g '*.go'
rg -n 'PayloadKeyCommand.*service|PayloadKeyCommand].*\(.*service|\.Payload\[events\.PayloadKeyCommand\].*\(.*service' internal/modules internal/pkg -g '*.go' --glob '!**/*_test.go'
rg -n 'internal/modules/kernel/(run|workspace|memory|decision|approval)/(biz|service)|repository\.(Run|Workspace|MemoryItem|DecisionTicket|Approval)Repository|ServiceExports|Set[A-Z].*Service|[A-Z].*Service\(\)' internal/modules/application/workorchd/biz/appservice -g '*.go' --glob '!**/*_test.go'
go test ./internal/modules/application/workorchd/... -count=1
```

Expected result:

- Appservice no longer acts as one large catch-all package for pure logic, DTOs, EventHub ports, runtime support, read-model projection, or owner command mapping.
- HTTP/service layer still depends only on application service facade or focused use-case packages.
- Low-level app DTOs, read-model DTOs, configuration, governance summary projection, localization rules, quality-check algorithms, skill selection algorithms, EventHub ports, and runtime support can move first while `appservice` preserves a thin facade for HTTP/CLI stability.
- Remaining larger appservice orchestration files are acceptable when they own a cohesive use-case boundary and keep all cross-owner interaction behind EventHub-backed ports.

### Phase 7: Background work audit

Tasks:

- Classify every `go func` as:
  - short-lived fan-out or local concurrency
  - long-lived listener/server
  - retry/recovery/continuation background task
- Move long-lived or retry/recovery work to BackgroundRoutine or a module-owned cancellable scheduler.
- Keep internal parallel fan-out only when it joins before returning.

Verification:

```bash
rg -n '\bgo func\b|AsyncFunction|AsyncTask|TimerWithContext|Timer\(' internal/modules internal/initiators application -g '*.go' --glob '!**/*_test.go'
go test ./internal/modules/... ./internal/pkg/... -count=1
WORKORCH_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:8080 make verify-ci-mvp-file
```

Expected result:

- Long-running work participates in framework shutdown.
- Request-local concurrency does not leak beyond request/run lifecycle.

Audit classification:

| Location | Category | Closure |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `internal/modules/kernel/orchestrator/biz/execution_context_package.go` | request-local fan-out, joined with `sync.WaitGroup` before return | acceptable |
| `internal/modules/kernel/orchestrator/biz/runtime_result_recording.go` | request-local artifact fan-out, joined through bounded result channels before return | acceptable |
| `internal/modules/kernel/orchestrator/biz/runtime_graph.go` | graph batch fan-out, joined with `sync.WaitGroup` before state reload | acceptable |
| `internal/modules/kernel/orchestrator/biz/runtime_run_state.go` | callback fan-out bounded by local task set and result collection | acceptable |
| `internal/modules/application/workorchd/biz/appservice/read_model_service.go` | read-model fan-out, joined with `sync.WaitGroup` before return | acceptable |
| `internal/modules/application/workorchd/biz/appservice/run_service.go` | request-local parallel owner reads, joined with `sync.WaitGroup` before return | acceptable |
| `internal/modules/application/workorchd/biz/appservice/runports/definition_validation.go` | request-local parallel owner reads, joined with `sync.WaitGroup` before return | acceptable |
| `internal/modules/application/workorchd/biz/appservice/workspace_execution_activation_service.go` | bounded parallel catalog/memory reads, joined with `sync.WaitGroup` before return | acceptable |
| `internal/modules/application/workorchd/biz/appservice/activationports/selection.go` | request-local parallel owner reads for selected MCP/memory refs, joined with `sync.WaitGroup` before return | acceptable |
| `internal/services/workorch/service/cli/agent_cmd.go` | command-local agent run goroutine; terminal branches now cancel context and wait on `runErr` before returning | acceptable |
| `internal/modules/application/workorchd/service/server/service.go` | HTTP server startup submitted to framework `BackgroundRoutine` | acceptable |
| `internal/modules/application/workorchd/service/server/http_types.go` | server-owned fallback scheduler uses cancellable context and `WaitGroup`; uses framework `BackgroundRoutine` when present | acceptable |
| `internal/modules/application/workorchd/service/server/http_run_cache.go` | scheduled cache warm task through server background scheduler; local timer observes context | acceptable |
| `internal/modules/application/workorchd/service/server/http_run_continuation_scheduler.go` | startup recovery, governance resume, and replan continuation use server background scheduler with cancellation and duplicate guards | acceptable |
| `internal/modules/kernel/governance/biz/service.go` | governance continuation/reconcile submitted to framework `BackgroundRoutine` when available; local timer observes cancellation | acceptable |
| `internal/modules/blocks/mcp/biz/mcp_runtime_adapter.go` | stdio session stderr/cmd wait/read goroutines are tied to process pipes and closed through session `Close` / command exit | acceptable |
| `internal/modules/blocks/mcp/biz/mcp_runtime_adapter.go` | stdio session stderr drain, process wait, and response reader goroutines are tied to session/context close | acceptable |
| `internal/services/workorch/service/cli/agent_cmd.go` | CLI command-local process stream handling | acceptable |

### Phase 8: Refresh framework/EventHub skills

Tasks:

- Refresh the relevant project/global skills after migration is complete.
- Make the skills state the WorkOrch-validated rules for:
  - entrypoint-only framework side-effect imports
  - initiator infrastructure-only boundaries
  - module `Setup` / `Run` / `Teardown` responsibilities
  - EventHub command/response for cross-owner state
  - shared runtime contract packages instead of owner module model imports
  - runtime injection through initiator only for infrastructure or narrow adapters
- Validate the updated skills with skill-hub before feeding them back.

Candidate skills:

- `go-multi-module-dev`
- `go-module-initiator-lifecycle`
- `go-application-event-runtime`

Verification:

```bash
skill-hub validate go-multi-module-dev --links
skill-hub validate go-module-initiator-lifecycle --links
skill-hub validate go-application-event-runtime --links
```

Expected result:

- Future applications can create and use `github.com/muidea/magicCommon/framework` modules consistently.
- Future applications can keep module/block communication on `github.com/muidea/magicCommon/event` EventHub contracts.
- Skills no longer encourage hidden framework imports, broad initiator service registries, or cross-owner direct repository/service access.

## Verification Matrix

| Area | Required check | Pass criteria |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Entrypoint | side-effect imports only in entrypoint or explicit assembly | no business package secretly enables modules |
| Initiator | no business startup governance | initiator exposes only infrastructure/runtime dependencies |
| System module | defaults initialization moved | `EnsureBuiltinDefaults` not called from initiator |
| Repository boundary | appservice no owner repo access | appservice uses EventHub-backed ports |
| Event contracts | topic files grouped | no giant mixed topic file growth |
| Command DTO | no service command leakage | event payload contracts live in pkg/contracts or pkg/events |
| Background work | long-lived work on BackgroundRoutine | shutdown can stop background tasks |
| E2E | deployed-instance checks | `make verify-ci-mvp-file` passes |

## Regression Commands

Run these after each phase:

```bash
git diff --check
go test ./internal/initiators/... ./internal/modules/... ./internal/pkg/... -count=1
go test ./application/workorch -count=1
```

Run deployed-instance regression after lifecycle or EventHub changes:

```bash
make run-file
WORKORCH_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:8080 make verify-ci-mvp-file
```

Stop the local service after verification.

## Migration Notes

- Do not use framework plugin manager to assemble embedded/local runtimes. Keep explicit lifecycle lists for embedded runtime.
- Do not introduce `ServiceExports`, `SetXService`, or `XService()` style registries.
- Do not move formal owner state into application or blocks.
- Do not make `Weight()` hide architecture dependencies; use it only for lifecycle ordering after dependencies are explicit.
- Prefer small compatibility-preserving moves first: dependency resolver, topic file split, system module extraction.

- Phase 6 closure criteria have been tightened: framework/EventHub boundary compliance is mandatory, while remaining same-package helper extraction is optional and should be driven by mixed responsibilities or boundary-risk reduction rather than line count. Current appservice structure verification shows focused subpackages for activation, app models, CLI, config, content, context registry, definitions, governance, localization, owner commands/reads, quality, read models, run/runtime, setup draft, skill selection, and workspace support. Generated empty test directories under `appservice/.workorch-data` and `appservice/workspaces` were removed before recording structure evidence.
- Latest Phase 6 mandatory boundary audit passed: `find internal/modules/application/workorchd/biz -maxdepth 2 -type d | sort`, `rg -n 'internal/modules/application/workorchd/biz/appservice' internal/modules/application/workorchd/service -g '*.go'`, `go test ./internal/modules/application/workorchd/... -count=1`, `git diff --check`, the service-command payload boundary scan, and the appservice direct owner repository/service import scan. The remaining files at or above 80 lines are optional maintainability candidates unless a later scan shows actual framework/EventHub boundary risk.
- Latest broader Phase 6 regression passed: `go test ./internal/initiators/... ./internal/modules/... ./internal/pkg/... -count=1`, `go test ./application/workorch -count=1`, and `git diff --check`.
- After upgrading to `magicCommon v1.5.9`, `application/workorch` now bridges framework `*cd.Error` returns through explicit nil checks before returning Go `error` values. This prevents typed-nil `error` interfaces from causing server mode to immediately shutdown after `application.Run`. Verification passed: `go test ./application/workorch ./internal/services/workorch/... -count=1`, `go test ./internal/modules/application/workorchd/... -count=1`, and `go test ./internal/initiators/... ./internal/modules/kernel/... ./internal/modules/blocks/... ./internal/pkg/... -count=1`.
